# Cross-Plan Dependency Graph and Ownership Map

## Purpose

Define the canonical build order, shared-resource ownership, and inter-plan dependencies so that implementation agents can execute plans in the correct sequence without discovering conflicts at build time.

## How to Use This Document

1. **Before starting any plan**: check its Dependencies row in the plan header. All listed plans must be complete.
2. **Before creating a table or directory**: check the Ownership Maps below. Only the owning plan issues `CREATE`. Dependent plans `EXTEND` with an explicit dependency declaration.
3. **Build order**: follow the Canonical Build Order (Section 5). Each tier's prerequisites are the prior tier's completion.

---

## 1. Table Ownership Map

These tables are claimed by multiple plans. The table below resolves each conflict.

| Table | Owning Plan (CREATE) | Extending Plan(s) (ALTER/USE) | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `session_memberships` | Plan-001 (Shared Session Core) | Plan-002 (Invite Membership And Presence) | Plan-001 is the session foundation; Spec-002 depends on Spec-001. Plan-001 creates the table with core columns (id, session_id, participant_id, role, state, joined_at, updated_at). Plan-002 adds invite-driven membership flows but does not own the schema. |
| `branch_contexts` | Plan-010 (Worktree Lifecycle And Execution Modes) | Plan-011 (Gitflow PR And Diff Attribution) | Plan-011 already declares Plan-010 as a dependency. Plan-010 creates the table as the polymorphic writable-root carrier (id, workspace_id FK NOT NULL, worktree_id FK nullable, ephemeral_clone_id FK nullable + at-most-one CHECK, base_branch, head_branch, upstream_ref, created_at, updated_at — Tier-6 audit, Plan-010 D-010-5). Plan-011 extends it for PR and diff attribution and imports `BranchContextId` per Plan-010 CP-010-6. |
| `participant_keys` (SQLite) | Plan-001 (initial migration `0001-initial.ts`) | Plan-022 (schema origin and CRUD code paths) | **Forward-declared split per Plan-022 header.** Plan-022 authors the `participant_keys` schema but forward-declares the `CREATE TABLE` into Plan-001's Tier 1 migration so V1 session-core cannot ship without the GDPR crypto-envelope schema (per ADR-015 V1 scope). Plan-022's implementation code paths (store, rotation, wrap-codec) land at Tier 5. |
| `session_events.pii_payload` (SQLite column) | Plan-001 (initial migration `0001-initial.ts`) | Plan-022 (column origin; **codec** code path at Tier 5) | **Forward-declared split per Plan-022 header.** Plan-022 adds this BLOB column to Plan-001's `session_events` schema in the Tier 1 migration so the crypto envelope does not require a breaking schema migration after V1 ships. **Write-mechanism vs codec (Tier-5 audit).** The sole **write-path** is Plan-006's `pii-indirection.ts` (Tier 4 — structural sole-write enforcement via the branded `PiiPayloadCiphertext` type, CP-006-1); Plan-022 owns the **codec** (Tier 5 — the AES-256-GCM encrypt/decrypt that produces/consumes the ciphertext the indirection layer writes). Distinct concerns: Plan-006 gates _who may write_ the column, Plan-022 supplies _the cipher_. |
| `session_events.{monotonic_ns,prev_hash,row_hash,daemon_signature,participant_signature}` (SQLite columns) | Plan-001 (initial migration `0001-initial.ts`) | Plan-006 (column origin; integrity-protocol semantics + writer/verifier code paths at Tier 4) | **Forward-declared split per `Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol`.** Plan-001 ships the physical CREATE of these columns in the Tier 1 initial migration so the integrity protocol does not require a breaking schema migration after V1 ships. Plan-006 owns the column semantics (monotonic-clock allocation, hash-chain construction, dual-signature mechanics) and the writer/verifier code paths at Tier 4. Plan-003 emits `runtime_node.*` events into this surface at Tier 3, but the events conform to the column shape Plan-001 ships — Plan-003 does not author the integrity-protocol semantics. |
| `daemon_signing_keys` (SQLite table, Plan-006-owned, sealed-Ed25519-private-key store) | Plan-006 (additive migration at Tier 4 Phase 2 — `0005-daemon-signing-keys.ts`; new local SQLite table) | Production composition root (leg A — invokes `DaemonSigningKeyProvisioner.create(sessionId)` at the daemon's local session-establishment per CP-006-7) + Plan-006 T4.10 (leg B — registers the returned public key in the control-plane `daemon_signing_public_keys` verification-key roster post-attach per CP-003-5) | **Plan-006 audit F-006-2-02 resolution (hardened-mode, user-ratified 2026-05-28); revised post-Codex T4 review on PR #124.** Daemon-private signing keys are local-machine secrets and MUST live in local SQLite (per ADR-004 SQLite-Local-State boundary). They are NEVER stored in shared Postgres `sessions` (the original pre-T4-review row mis-located the column there — corrected to a new local-SQLite table here). Plan-006 Phase 2 T2.7 ships the `DaemonSigningKeySource` interface + `OsKeystoreSealedDaemonSigningKeySource` implementation, which generates the per-session Ed25519 keypair and seals the private half through an INJECTED `DaemonSigningKeySealer` boundary rather than sealing it itself; stored as ciphertext in the new local `daemon_signing_keys.sealed_private_key` BLOB column keyed by `session_id`. Plan-022 (Tier 5) ships that sealer AND specifies the column's byte format per CP-006-11 — sealing under the OS-keystore-managed master key (Keychain `kSecAttrAccessibleWhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly` on macOS / `CRED_TYPE_GENERIC` `CRED_PERSIST_LOCAL_MACHINE` on Windows / Secret Service via libsecret + kwallet6 + keyutils fallback on Linux per [Spec-022 §Daemon Master Key](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#daemon-master-key)), accessed through the `@napi-rs/keyring` v1.2.0 cross-platform shim (`keytar` superseded — unmaintained). No tier inversion: the Plan-022 dependency runs through the injected interface, so Plan-006's module imports nothing from Plan-022 and pulls no native binding into the append path's consumers. (The 2026-05-28 ratification reached the same no-inversion conclusion from the stronger premise of no Plan-022 dependency at all; that premise is superseded in part 2026-07-27, PR #256 — the conclusion is not.) **Re-vehicled 2026-07-29 (the CP-006-7 two-leg correction).** The former "Plan-002 (Tier 1, closed) reopens via amendment-via-extension PR per CP-002-N" clause was a double misattribution carrying an unminted placeholder id — Plan-002 (Tier 2, invites/membership/presence) owns no session-create surface and its file never carried a reciprocal. The call-site (leg A) binds at the first production composition root that wires T3.1's durable append path, in the CP-006-1 assertion-pattern shape with T3.1's null-key fail-closed throw as backstop; the roster public-key registration (leg B) is Plan-006 T4.10 post-attach — a new idempotent control-plane procedure pair (`runtimenode.signingkeyregister` / `runtimenode.signingkeyroster`) over the new Plan-006-owned erasure-durable `daemon_signing_public_keys` table per CP-003-5, register-once with different-key refusal; no shipped attach wire shape or `runtime_node_attachments` column is touched (no master-key crypto crosses either boundary; `create` returns the public key only). `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` (the daemon public key is resolved from the session participant roster by NodeId for signature verification) is the governing semantic for EXTERNAL verifiers; the daemon-local verifier (T4.1) checking rows it authored resolves its own `session_id`-keyed store — the NodeId = self arm. **Companion delivery table (T4.10 targeted readiness-audit delta; Codex PR #278 rounds 2–3, 2026-08-01):** the restart-survivable copy of the attach response's server-minted attachment binding the T4.10 registrar's attempt precondition consumes lives in the dedicated `daemon_attachment_deliveries` table (`0NNN-attachment-deliveries.ts`, registered through the daemon migration-runner's `hasMigrationApplied`-guarded-block seam in the same commit as the migration file) — round 3 re-designed it OFF this table, whose NOT NULL key columns admit no delivery-only row, so delivery durability never waits on key provisioning; this table stays key-material-only with T2.7's module its sole production write path, and the delivery table's sole write path is `attachment-delivery-store.ts` (handler-committed before acknowledgment — acked-means-durable). |
| `session_events.retention_class` (SQLite column, `TEXT` nullable; `'audit_stub'` discriminator) | Plan-006 (additive migration at Tier 4 Phase 3 — `0NNN-retention-class-and-stub-signature.ts`) | None (Plan-006-internal: compactor T3.2 writes; replay-service T4.3 reads; verifier T4.1 keys compacted-row verification off it) | **Plan-006 audit F-006-3-02 resolution (hardened-mode, Phase 3 Design B).** Compactor (T3.2) sets `retention_class = 'audit_stub'` after compacting a row's payload to the audit-stub form per [Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#compacted-event-format). A typed column (vs JSON-field probe inside `payload`) keeps hot-path replay-window construction fast and lets the compactor selector exclude already-stubbed rows via `WHERE retention_class IS NULL` for re-compaction guards. Partial index `WHERE retention_class IS NULL` recommended for compactor candidate-range queries. |
| `session_events.stub_signature` (SQLite column, `BLOB` nullable; 64-byte Ed25519) | Plan-006 (additive migration at Tier 4 Phase 3 — `0NNN-retention-class-and-stub-signature.ts`, same migration as `retention_class`) | None (Plan-006-internal: compactor T3.2 writes; verifier T4.1 re-verifies) | **Plan-006 post-compaction stub-authenticity resolution (hardened-mode; Codex PR #124 round-2 P1, 2026-05-28).** Per-row commitment to the surviving audit-stub bytes: the compactor (T3.2) signs `canonical_bytes(audit-stub projection)` with the daemon session key and stores the 64-byte signature here; the verifier (T4.1) re-verifies it against the row's CURRENT stub bytes — tampered/replayed/missing → `failureMode: 'stub_signature_invalid'`. Closes the gap where the round-1 anchor (which commits only to the ORIGINAL `row_hash`) could not detect a post-compaction edit of the visible stub. A companion **scalar-binding check** (verifier T4.1; Codex PR #124 round-5 P1, 2026-05-28) additionally binds the surviving scalar columns (`category`/`type`/`actor`/`occurred_at`, plus `id`/`session_id`/`sequence`) to the signed `payload` projection — a scalar edited to diverge from the projection (which `stub_signature` alone cannot catch, since it signs only `payload`) → `failureMode: 'stub_scalar_mismatch'`. NULL for live (`retention_class IS NULL`) rows. See [Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#post-compaction-integrity). |
| `session_events.pii_participant_id` (SQLite column, `TEXT` nullable; the durable PII owner stamp) | Plan-006 (additive migration at Tier 4 Phase 3 — `0NNN-pii-participant-id.ts`, T3.1's own append-path migration, distinct from the T3.2 compaction migration above) | Plan-022 (read-only consumer at Path-1 shred — `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out` Path 1's SQL scope selector keys off this stamp rather than the opaque ciphertext — and bound by CP-006-12 to preserve it; no ALTER, no write) | **Plan-006 PII-owner-stamp binding resolution (hardened-mode; Codex PR #256 round-4 P1, 2026-07-27).** The durable half of the owner stamp: T2.4's `pii-indirection.ts` projects the PII owner's participant id into the signed `payload` under `PII_PARTICIPANT_ID_PAYLOAD_KEY`, and T3.1's append path writes that SAME id into this column on the same write — so `daemon_signature` binds WHOSE ciphertext a row holds, not only WHICH bytes (the `pii_ciphertext_digest` half, I-006-2-11). Without the column the signed claim is its own only copy and the binding is unenforceable: T2.4's exported `isPiiOwnerStampBound` compares column against claim, and T4.1 runs it as a POSTCONDITION of a green `verifyRow` — the claim lives inside the bytes that check authenticates — reporting `failureMode: 'pii_owner_stamp_unbound'` per I-006-2-12. NULL on every row with no `pii_payload`. What the binding buys is post-shred attribution: once [Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#shred-fan-out) Path 1 destroys the per-participant key no decrypt can re-attribute the retained ciphertext, so the stamp is at once the sole surviving evidence of whose data the row held and Path 1's own scope selector — which is why CP-006-12 binds Plan-022 to preserve BOTH `pii_payload` and this column. Whether a COMPACTED row retains the stamp is T3.1's migration to settle (the signed CLAIM always retires — the stub projection replaces `payload` wholesale with a field set carrying no PII members); the recorded expectation is to clear the column alongside `pii_payload`. See [Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#canonical-serialization-rules), which assigns the column to T3.1 by name. |
| `session_snapshots.{has_compacted_ranges,compacted_range_count}` (SQLite columns) | Plan-006 (additive migration at Tier 4 Phase 4 — `0NNN-session-snapshots-compaction-cursor.ts`) | Plan-015 (read-only consumer at recovery dispatch — flag tells recovery dispatcher whether replay across this snapshot range will encounter audit stubs) | **Plan-006 audit F-006-4-01 resolution (hardened-mode, Phase 4 Reading (a)).** `has_compacted_ranges BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` and `compacted_range_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` are read-side hints that replay output across this snapshot's `as_of_sequence` range will encounter audit stubs. NOT redundant with Plan-015's separate `replay_cursors.last_sequence` (different question: replay_cursors = projector resumption pointer; these flags = compaction-incidence indicator). Plan-006 Phase 4 T4.4 adds the columns. [Session Snapshots](schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md#session-snapshots-plan-001-extended-by-plans-006-015) already lists Plan-006 as an explicit extender of `session_snapshots`. |
| `participants` (Postgres) | Plan-001 (initial migration `0001-initial.ts` — minimal `id UUID PK`, `created_at TIMESTAMPTZ`) | Plan-018 (Identity And Participant State — additive ALTER migrations for `display_name`, `identity_ref`, `metadata` + the `identity_mappings` side table) | **Forward-declared split per [Plan-001 §Cross-Plan Forward-Declared Schema](../plans/001-shared-session-core.md#cross-plan-forward-declared-schema).** Plan-001 owns the physical CREATE of the minimal identity-anchor shape at Tier 1 because `session_memberships.participant_id`, `session_invites.inviter_id`, and `runtime_node_attachments.participant_id` all `REFERENCES participants(id)` (Plans 001/002/003 execute before Plan-018 per §5 Canonical Build Order). Plan-018 extends with identity/profile columns and the `identity_mappings` side table via additive ALTER migrations at Tier 5. |
| `sessions.min_client_version` (Postgres column) | Plan-001 (initial migration `0001-initial.ts` — `min_client_version TEXT` forward-decl, NULL = no floor) | Plan-003 (Runtime Node Attach — attach-time version-floor enforcement per `ADR-018 §Decision` #4) | **Forward-declared split per [Plan-001 §Cross-Plan Forward-Declared Schema](../plans/001-shared-session-core.md#cross-plan-forward-declared-schema).** Plan-001 ships the column shape (TEXT, NULL default = no floor) at Tier 1 so V1 session-core can persist a per-session client-version floor without a breaking schema migration. `ADR-018 §Decision` #4 routes the floor through Plan-003's runtime-node-attach flow: peers read the floor from this column at attach time and reject below-floor writes with the `VERSION_FLOOR_EXCEEDED` envelope (shipped at Plan-001 T2.3 / `packages/contracts/src/error.ts`). Control-plane is authoritative for session metadata per [ADR-004](../decisions/004-sqlite-local-state-and-postgres-control-plane.md). Plan-001 PR #4 does NOT write the column; Plan-003 picks up read/write semantics at Tier 3 per BL-090. |
| `command_receipts` (SQLite) | Plan-004 (Queue, Steer, Pause, Resume) | Plan-015 (recovery + two-phase idempotency protocol, BL-051 columns); Plan-005 (additive nullable `mcp_task_id`, campaign B10) | Plan-004 CREATEs the table (`id`, `command_id`, `run_id`, `status`, `created_at`) as the crash-recovery command-receipt log. Plan-015 EXTENDs with the BL-051 two-phase-commit columns (`idempotency_class`, `dedupe_key`, `started_at`, `completed_at`) for recovery dispatch. Plan-005 EXTENDs with the additive nullable `mcp_task_id` column **via its own Plan-005 migration** (never a Plan-004 migration edit, never reshaping Plan-004's columns): the receiver-generated MCP Tasks `taskId` (from the `CreateTaskResult` acceptance response) that Spec-015 recovery polls via `tasks/get` + `tasks/result` instead of halting the `manual_reconcile_only` floor (Plan-005 T5.1 — the gated Phase 5, machine-ordered after Plan-004 Phase 1's CREATE; the T3.13 receipt-write seam ships dormant until it lands). Canonical DDL: [local-sqlite-schema.md §Queue and Intervention Tables (Plan-004)](schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md#queue-and-intervention-tables-plan-004). |

| Plan-028 | `mcp_server_trust`, `mcp_tool_overrides`, `mcp_mutation_receipts` (SQLite; campaign B18 — the config-hash-bound operator trust store (keyed per-binding hashing salt), per-tool override store, and governance-mutation idempotency receipt store per Spec-028, untrusted-by-default with drift auto-revoke; DDL in local-sqlite-schema.md §MCP Governance Tables (Plan-028)) |

### Lock Ordering Across Shared Tables

Plans that touch more than one of the §1 contested or co-extended tables in a single transaction MUST acquire row locks in the order documented below to avoid cross-plan deadlocks. Adding a new caller is not sufficient grounds to deviate; reordering requires a coordinated change to all callers under the affected order.

| Tables Locked Together | Order | Authority | Affected Callers |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `sessions`, `session_memberships` | `sessions` → `session_memberships` | Plan-001 docstring at [`packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-directory-service.ts`](../../packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-directory-service.ts) (`createSession` "Lock-acquisition order" paragraph) | Plan-002 ownership-transfer, co-owner promotion, and invite-accept paths (any flow that mutates `session_memberships` while validating `sessions`) |
| `sessions`, `session_memberships`, `runtime_node_attachments`, `daemon_signing_public_keys` | `sessions` → `session_memberships` → `runtime_node_attachments` → `daemon_signing_public_keys` | Plan-006 T4.10 register transaction (registered 2026-07-31 at the CP-006-7 amendment, PR #274 round 3; `session_memberships` joined at round 4's active-membership predicate; lands with `signing-key-service.ts`). `sessions` is taken explicitly FIRST even though only read: the final INSERT's `session_id` FK acquires an implicit `FOR KEY SHARE` on `sessions`, and skipping a level in a total order is sound only when nothing later in the transaction acquires the skipped level implicitly — the same rule the shipped attach path applies. Modes are weakest-sufficient: `sessions` `FOR SHARE` (conflicts with a future floor raise's `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`; `FOR KEY SHARE` would be a silent no-op guard), `session_memberships` `FOR SHARE` (an authorization READ — the transaction writes nothing to that table, so Plan-003 I-003-3 holds; `FOR SHARE` is the weakest mode conflicting with the `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` a `MembershipService` suspend/revoke `UPDATE` acquires, so a revocation cannot commit between the authorization read and the key INSERT; no deadlock against `updateMembership`, which serializes at level 1 on its `sessions` `FOR UPDATE`), `runtime_node_attachments` `FOR SHARE` (conflicts with detach's state UPDATE) | Plan-006 T4.10 `runtimenode.signingkeyregister` only in V1 — since round 4 it instantiates the full four-level order whose level-2 slot the BL-141 campaign design reserved: the design's runtime-node mutator extension joins the same `session_memberships` `FOR SHARE` level when it lands (same relative order, no re-ordering) |

## 2. Package Path Ownership Map

These directories or files are targeted by multiple plans. The owning plan creates the directory; dependent plans add files within it.

| Path | Owning Plan (CREATE) | Extending Plan(s) | Files Added by Extending Plans |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/` | Plan-001 (Tier 1 — `session-directory-service.ts` shipped at Phase 4) | Plan-008 (Tier 1 bootstrap-deliverable + Tier 5 remainder) + the migration-registrant seam on `migration-runner.ts` (every plan shipping a control-plane migration; see the `migrations/` row below) | Plan-008-bootstrap (Tier 1): `session-router.ts` (tRPC `sessionRouter` HTTP handlers wrapping the existing `session-directory-service.ts`), `session-subscribe-sse.ts` (SSE substrate for `SessionSubscribe` per `packages/contracts/src/session.ts#SessionSubscribeRequest`), `session-join-service.ts` (initial Tier 1 stub if needed by Phase 5; otherwise extends at Tier 5). Plan-008-remainder (Tier 5): full join-service + reconnect-association integration with relay-broker-service. Plan-001 owns the directory and the directory-service file; Plan-008 extends with HTTP/SSE transport and join orchestration without modifying `session-directory-service.ts` semantics. ### Ownership Rule

The owning plan creates the directory structure and any shared types or base services. Extending plans add new files but must not modify files owned by the creating plan without an explicit contract (interface, type export, or extension point).

**Housekeeping Exception.** An extending plan MAY fix-in-place an owned file in the surfacing PR (rather than serializing through a precursor PR owned by the creating plan) IFF:

- (a) the defect is type-level or structural-only with zero runtime or behavioral change,
- (b) the defect was first discoverable via the extending plan's composition (the extender is the first surfacer),
- (c) the fix is sub-30-LOC + sub-day in scope,
- (d) the change is one-time correction — not an ongoing edit claim on the owned file, AND
- (e) the exception is documented inline in the extending plan's `§Cross-Plan Amendments` section, with rationale citing which of (a)–(d) apply.

The intent is to avoid cross-plan PR-coordination ceremony for sub-day, low-risk fixes that the extender's own work surfaced. Rigid serialization through a precursor PR remains the default for behavioral changes, multi-day fixes, or anything that claims ongoing edit rights on the owned file. A §6 NS-XX cross-reference entry is RECOMMENDED when the cross-plan touch is load-bearing for downstream dependency planning, but is not required for purely tactical type-fix amendments.

Behavioral changes to shipped owned code are not covered by this exception — they take the post-completion enhancement lane (CONTRIBUTING.md §How Code Lands lane 2), or a spec/plan amendment first when they alter the behavior contract (lane 4).

**Precedent.** [Plan-002 §Cross-Plan Amendments](../plans/002-invite-membership-and-presence.md#cross-plan-amendments) records PR #102's Amendment 1 (`packages/contracts/src/internal/branded.ts` extraction + `packages/contracts/src/session.ts` refactor — type-level only, surfaced during T1.1 implementation, sub-30-LOC) and Amendment 2 (`packages/control-plane/src/sessions/migration-runner.ts` per-version-loop wiring — structural-only, surfaced during Phase D code review, sub-30-LOC).

## 3. Inter-Plan Dependency Graph

Each dependency is annotated with its type:

- **spec-declared**: the corresponding spec explicitly lists the other spec in its Depends On field
- **implementation-derived**: the plans share tables, package paths, or cross-cutting concerns that create a build-order dependency not captured in spec Depends On
- **declared in plan header**: the dependency is declared in the target plan's header Dependencies row — not in the spec's Depends On field, and not an implementation-derived table-sharing relationship

## 4. Plans With No Inter-Plan Dependencies

These plans depend only on domain models and architecture docs, not on other plans. They can begin as early as their tier in the build order allows.

| Plan | Why Standalone |
| --- | --- |
| Plan-001 | Session foundation — everything depends on this. _PR-level exception:_ Plan-001 Phase 5 (`sessionClient`) consumes Plan-007-partial + Plan-008-bootstrap + Plan-023-partial as Tier 1 substrate prerequisites — see §3 row and §5 Tier 1 carve-out. Phase 1–Phase 4 remain dependency-free. |
| Plan-007 | Local IPC — daemon control surface, no shared tables, no upstream-as-blocker plan dependencies. _Phase-level import:_ Plan-007 Phase 3 imports Plan-001 Phase 2 (`packages/contracts/src/session.ts` + `packages/contracts/src/event.ts`) — already shipped at Tier 1, so no actual blocking. _Carve-out:_ Plan-007-partial (`Spec-007 §Wire Format` substrate + `session.*` namespace + SDK Zod layer) is a Tier 1 prerequisite for Plan-001 Phase 5 — see §5 Tier 1 carve-out and `Plan-007 §Execution Windows (V1 Carve-Out)`. |
| Plan-009 | Workspace binding — no upstream FEATURE-plan blocker, but not self-contained: earlier-tier substrate/taxonomy edges per the §3 row (Plan-001, Plan-003, Plan-006 emitter precondition, Plan-007-partial, Plan-023-partial — all complete before Tier 6); no shared tables with earlier plans |
| Plan-012 | Approvals/permissions — no upstream FEATURE-plan blocker, but the approval model is NOT self-contained: substrate and schema edges per the §3 row (Plan-001/003/004/005/006/007-partial/009/010/023-partial — Tier-6 audit). _Spec-level integration:_ Spec-024 cross-node dispatch surface consumes Plan-012's approval categories; Plan-027 owns the Spec-024 V1 implementation per §Spec-024 Implementation Plan in §5. _Campaign gate:_ the allow-listed spawn-env invariant (I-012-20), the resident-authorizer no-decision-cache invariant (I-012-21), the driver-ask normalizer + posture-authorization task set, and the empirical WASM latency benchmark landed via the campaign's B13 bundle (Task 16, merged 2026-07-20), which flipped Plan-012 `approved → review` (restored at its W2.5 targeted re-audit); the pre-B13 approved plan carried the pre-campaign scope only (ADR-012 2026-07-02) — see the §5 build-order preamble. |
| Plan-014 | Artifacts — no upstream FEATURE-plan blocker, but not self-contained: earlier-tier substrate/taxonomy edges per the §3 row (Plan-006 emitter precondition for the `artifact_publication` taxonomy + `EventLogService.append`; Plan-007-partial `artifact.*` IPC registry substrate; Plan-008-bootstrap tRPC host + Plan-008-remainder R4 relay-crypto channels (Tier 5 — the 2026-07-09 artifact-key attestation transport) + Plan-025 Tier 1 Partial `crypto-paseto` + Plan-021 rate-limit middleware (Tier 6, BL-146 self-wiring) — all complete before Tier 7); no shared tables with earlier plans (Tier-7 audit 2026-06-15). _2026-07-08 scope growth:_ the cross-node relay pull-forward (ADR-015 amendment) adds Tasks 7–10, the two Postgres `artifact_relay_*` tables, and the daemon-local `artifact_encryption_keys` durable-key store (§1 row) — the Tier-7 audit covers Tasks 1–6 only, so Tasks 7–10 dispatch only after Plan-014's readiness-audit delta (plan re-opened `review`); reciprocals: CP-014-2 ⇄ Plan-022 CP-022-6 erasure closure, CP-014-3 → Plan-025 deployment composition. |

## 5. Canonical Build Order

Implementation must follow this tier sequence. Plans in a tier can generally run in parallel, but some tiers contain intra-tier ordering noted in the Prerequisites column. All plans in a tier must complete before the next tier begins. Tier sequencing governs plan-task shipment (CONTRIBUTING.md §How Code Lands lane 1); post-completion enhancements and repo tooling changes are not tier-gated.

V1 scope is 23 features per [ADR-015: V1 Feature Scope Definition](../decisions/015-v1-feature-scope-definition.md) (amended 2026-07-02 per the capability-enhancement campaign — 17 features per the earlier 2026-04-22 BL-097 workflow V1.1→V1 amendment; amended 2026-07-08 — cross-node shared artifacts pulled from V1.1 into V1 as feature-14 scope growth, landing as Plan-014 Tasks 7–10 gated on that plan's readiness-audit delta per the §6 Plan-014 row). All tiers below are the V1 tier set; feature #18's governing plan ([Plan-028](../plans/028-mcp-server-configuration-and-governance.md)) landed 2026-07-22 via the campaign's B18 bundle (Task 24) and holds its Tier 7 row below — a Tier-7 leaf (every gating upstream edge resolves by Tier 6 end; the one within-Tier-7 edge — Plan-015's recovery-attach admission seam — is order-independent by its vacuous default, and nothing depends on Plan-028) whose code dispatches only after its targeted readiness audit promotes it out of `draft` per its §Preconditions. Features #19–#23 are likewise doc-gated: their governing spec amendments are in-tree via the campaign's B1/B2/B3/B6/B20 bundles and were re-promoted `approved` through the W1.5 batch gate (campaign Task 28, 2026-07-18) — Spec-004, Spec-012, and Spec-016 re-flipped `review` 2026-08-03 by the V1 product-vision reconciliation bundle, each awaiting its restoring targeted readiness-audit delta, so the spec-layer doc gate on #19–#23 is re-opened on those three (Spec-005 and Spec-006 remain `approved`) — and the Plan-004 / Plan-005 / Plan-012 / Plan-016 rows below still cover those plans' pre-campaign scope until each feature's W2 plan-task bundle (B9 / B10 / B13 / B15) lands — rollback / goals / callback-tool / execution-posture / realtime work dispatches only after its W2 bundle merges, not on tier order alone. **Rollback (#19) additionally gates on an implementation leg beyond its spec amendments:** the daemon-side turn-snapshot **file-restore** path ([Plan-010](../plans/010-worktree-lifecycle-and-execution-modes.md), campaign B21→B22→B23 / Task 26) must land **before Plan-004 Phase 3** dispatches rollback — otherwise `run.rolled_back` could emit with the worktree unrestored (conversation reverted, files not), per [ADR-015](../decisions/015-v1-feature-scope-definition.md) feature #19 and [v1-feature-scope.md](./v1-feature-scope.md) row #19. **Local-log-authoritative resume (ADR-017 P1-5) gated [Plan-015](../plans/015-persistence-recovery-and-replay.md)'s divergence handling:** the divergent-provider-position reconciliation (divergence ⇒ HALT-FOR-HUMAN, no silent accept or discard) landed in [Spec-015](../specs/015-persistence-recovery-and-replay.md) §Fallback via the campaign's B5 bundle (merged 2026-07-06; Spec-015 re-promoted `approved` 2026-07-18 via the W1.5 batch gate) and in Plan-015 via the campaign's B14 bundle (Task 17, merged 2026-07-21), which authored the T15.5 position-compare + HALT-FOR-HUMAN task beside T15.3's un-resumable-handle arm — an additive amendment, no flip; the W2.5 targeted re-audit closed attestation-only (Status unchanged `approved`). The divergence scope is now in-plan, no longer B14-gated — Plan-015's resume code dispatches on tier order (Tier 7) plus its §Preconditions — and T15.5 lives in the same plan as the resume tasks because shipping resume without it would silently accept or discard a divergent provider position against the [ADR-017](../decisions/017-shared-event-sourcing-scope.md) ruling. **Orphan-process defense (ADR-019 P2-2/P3-5) gated [Plan-024](../plans/024-rust-pty-sidecar.md)'s V1 completeness:** the I-024-1/I-024-2 Windows kill wire-through, the hardened two-layer orphan registry, both resync tiers (R3 — previously criterion-gated-deferred), the Spec-003 shared-terminal write-lease leg, and real `signal_code` observation landed in Plan-024 via the campaign's B16 bundle (Task 18, 2026-07-21), which adds **Phase 3B** (T-024-3B-1..6, invariants I-024-7..12) between Phase 3 and Phase 4 and flipped Plan-024 `approved → review`. The orphan-safety scope is now in-plan, no longer B16-gated; Phase 3B code dispatches on its own §Preconditions (Phase 3 merged, plus — registered 2026-08-02, CP-024-5 / PR #279 — Plan-006 Phase 3 merged: the T-024-3B-3 lease publisher types against Plan-006 T3.3's `DaemonCredentialProvider`, so Phase 3B holds a Tier-4 row in the table below as the deferred remainder leg of this Tier-1 plan — the Plan-007-partial/remainder shape, keeping the strict tier-completion rule intact; the 2026-08-02 CP-024-5 amendment also flipped Plan-024 `approved → review`, restored `approved` 2026-08-03 by the CP-024-5 targeted readiness-audit delta — PR #283 / §6 NS-44) — Plan-024's W2.5 re-promotion landed 2026-07-21 (flipped and restored `approved` the same day; PR #241) — and the Windows-default flip is machine-gated on Phase 3B by Plan-024's Phase-5 `preconditions:` block — so the default flip and Plan-005's consumption inherit the V1 orphan-safety the [ADR-019](../decisions/019-windows-v1-tier-and-pty-sidecar.md) 2026-07-02 amendment requires. **Resident-authorizer residency + no-decision-cache (ADR-012 P1-7) gated [Plan-012](../plans/012-approvals-permissions-and-trust-boundaries.md)'s permission-check scope:** ADR-012's 2026-07-02 amendment makes V1 `PermissionCheck` evaluation per-request with **no decision cache** (a decision is never served stale) and ties the `<1ms p95` latency target to an empirical end-to-end WASM benchmark; the no-cache invariant (I-012-21) + that benchmark task (T2.11) — together with the allow-listed spawn-env invariant (I-012-20), the T1.5/T2.8 driver-ask normalization leg, and the posture-authorization tasks — landed in Plan-012 via the campaign's B13 bundle (Task 16, merged 2026-07-20), which flipped Plan-012 `approved → review`. The no-cache / WASM-benchmark scope is now in-plan, no longer B13-gated; the 2026-07-20 flip was restored `approved` by Plan-012's W2.5 targeted re-audit (2026-07-21, PR #240), so Plan-012 code dispatches on tier order (Tier 6), the plan's §Preconditions, and its phase `preconditions:` Gate-5 chains. **Append-substrate enrichment gated [Plan-006](../plans/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md)'s Phase 3–4 dispatch:** the campaign's B11 bundle (Task 15) extended the existing T3.1 and T4.3 deliverables — `withSessionAppendLock` plus the terminal-backstop partial unique index on T3.1, `timelineCursors.earliest` cursor semantics on T4.3, and the event-kind disposition table with its T1.8 exhaustiveness test — contracts [Plan-004](../plans/004-queue-steer-pause-resume.md) and [Plan-008](../plans/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md) consume; landed via B11 (merged 2026-07-20), so Plan-006's Phase 3 and Phase 4 are now dispatch-eligible on tier order + each phase's §Preconditions, no longer B11-gated (the plan-level Status gate that additionally held dispatch from 2026-07-29 — the CP-006-7 T4.10 amendment's `approved → review` flip — cleared 2026-08-01 when the targeted readiness-audit delta restored `approved`, PR #278 / NS-42 — re-engaged 2026-08-02, when the PR #281 T3.1 ingest-halt seam amendment re-flipped Plan-006 `approved → review`, and cleared the same day when the T3.1-seam targeted readiness-audit delta restored `approved` (PR #282 / NS-43)). Plans outside the V1 set are listed in the V1.1+ subsection after this table.

### Plan-007 Substrate-vs-Namespace Carve-Out (Tier 1 / Tier 4)

Plan-001 Phase 5 (`packages/client-sdk/src/sessionClient.ts` `create` / `read` / `join` / `subscribe` over the daemon transport) consumes the Spec-007 wire substrate (JSON-RPC 2.0 + LSP-style Content-Length framing, 1MB max-message-size, protocol-version negotiation, error model, supervision hooks per `Spec-007 §Wire Format`) plus the `session.*` JSON-RPC method namespace. Spec-007 conflates two concerns — the cross-cutting _transport substrate_ (correctly single-owned by Plan-007) and the domain-specific _method namespaces_ (`session.*`, `run.*`, `repo.*`, `artifact.*`, `settings.*`, `daemon.*`) which cohere with their owning plans. **Resolution: Plan-007-partial** — the `Spec-007 §Wire Format` substrate (`packages/runtime-daemon/src/ipc/local-ipc-gateway.ts`, `protocol-negotiation.ts`), the bind-time Spec-027 substrate (`packages/runtime-daemon/src/bootstrap/secure-defaults.ts`, `packages/runtime-daemon/src/bootstrap/secure-defaults-events.ts` — validation scope limited to the loopback OS-local socket bind path Tier 1 exposes), the `session.*` method namespace only, and the SDK Zod layer (~500–1000 LOC per `Spec-007 §Wire Format`) — lands at Tier 1 alongside Plan-001 to unblock Phase 5. **Plan-007-remainder** (the `settings.*` + `daemon.*` JSON-RPC namespaces, the Spec-027 surfaces that widen alongside additional bind paths — `tls-surface.ts`, `first-run-keys.ts`, `update-notify.ts`, and the CLI `self-update` dual-verification command — and the CLI + desktop-shell supervision surfaces) ships at the original Tier 4 slot. The `run.*` / `repo.*` / `artifact.*` namespaces are owned by their downstream plans — Plan-004 (Tier 5) / Plan-009 (Tier 6) / Plan-014 (Tier 7) — and register their handlers under Plan-007's namespace registry at the consuming plan's tier per the Plan-002 `presence.*` precedent (NS-26), exactly as Plan-005's `driver.*` and Plan-006's `event.*` do (§3): Plan-007 owns the registry surface, not these namespaces' handlers. The `session.*` namespace is not duplicated at Tier 4; Plan-007-remainder extends the registry with its `settings.*` + `daemon.*` namespaces.

This establishes the **substrate-vs-namespace decomposition pattern** as a reusable methodology rule — see `Plan-007 §Execution Windows (V1 Carve-Out)`. The rule: cross-cutting infrastructure plans MAY be split into a Tier-N substrate-deliverable + a Tier-M namespace-deliverable when (a) the substrate is single-owned and load-bearing for an earlier-tier consumer, AND (b) the namespaces have natural cohesion with their owning plans. Substrate ships first; namespaces ship with their owning plans.

Operational admission for substrate phases routes through the `audit_status: substrate_exempt` phase-level precondition per [runbook §Per-Phase Audit Semantics](../operations/plan-implementation-readiness-audit-runbook.md#per-phase-audit-semantics). A substrate phase qualifies only when (1) it is covered by a §5 carve-out, (2) it ships substrate-only files, AND (3) it covers no Spec-NNN acceptance criteria (criterion (3) is the mechanically-checked operative filter — preflight rejects substrate claims that ship `Spec coverage:` cites in their Tasks block). The plan-level audit checkbox stays unchecked until the plan's later-tier remainder audit completes; until then the plan stays in `approved` status with the checkbox deferred via the `substrate_exempt` declaration on the affected phases.

### Plan-008 Bootstrap-vs-Remainder Carve-Out (Tier 1 / Tier 5)

Plan-001 Phase 5's `sessionClient.subscribe` over the control-plane transport requires the tRPC v11 server skeleton plus an SSE transport for `SessionSubscribe` (the contract is request-only on the wire — the response is an `AsyncIterable<EventEnvelope>` SSE stream per `packages/contracts/src/session.ts#SessionSubscribeRequest`). Plan-001 Phase 4 already shipped the underlying `packages/control-plane/src/sessions/session-directory-service.ts` at Tier 1; what's missing is the HTTP transport that exposes it. **Resolution: Plan-008-bootstrap** — the tRPC v11 server skeleton (Cloudflare Workers via `@trpc/server/adapters/fetch` per [ADR-014](../decisions/014-trpc-control-plane-api.md) and BL-104 resolution 2026-04-30; local dev uses workerd via `wrangler dev`), the `sessionRouter` HTTP handlers wrapping the existing `session-directory-service.ts`, and the SSE substrate for `SessionSubscribe` (produced natively by tRPC's shared `resolveResponse.ts` substrate when invoked through `fetchRequestHandler`) — lands at Tier 1 alongside Plan-001 to unblock Phase 5. **Plan-008-remainder** (relay broker, presence register, invite-acceptance handoff, reconnect association, `session_directory` + `relay_connections` + `relay_seen_ephemeral_keys` Postgres tables) ships at the original Tier 5 slot once Plan-002 (invite/presence) is complete. Tier 5 placement of Plan-008-remainder is unchanged because relay coordination depends on Plan-002.

This carve-out follows the same substrate-vs-namespace decomposition rule as Plan-007 above (cross-reference `Plan-007 §Execution Windows (V1 Carve-Out)`): the _transport substrate_ (tRPC server + sessionRouter + SSE plumbing) is what Plan-001 Phase 5 consumes; the _relay/presence behavior_ is what Plan-008 owns canonically. Substrate ships first.

### Plan-023 Substrate-vs-Namespace Carve-Out (Tier 1 / Tier 8)

Plan-001 Phase 5 (`apps/desktop/src/main/sidecar-lifecycle.ts` per CP-001-1 + `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/session-bootstrap/` per T5.2) and Plan-024 Phase 3 (I-024-4 satisfaction integration test at `apps/desktop/src/main/__tests__/sidecar-lifecycle.integration.test.ts`) both extend file content into the `apps/desktop/` directory tree. Plan-023 (Tier 8 canonical) owns that tree — but the tree's bare existence (workspace package + electron-vite toolchain + minimal main/preload/renderer entrypoints) is what Plan-001 Phase 5 + Plan-024 Phase 3 actually consume; the heavy main-process behavior (daemon supervisor, keystore, WebAuthn, auto-updater, deep-link, crash reporter) is what Plan-023 owns canonically and depends on Plan-007 / Plan-018 / Plan-008 / Plan-024 — none of which are at Tier 1. Spec-023 conflates two concerns the same way Spec-007 / Spec-008 did: the cross-cutting _package skeleton substrate_ (correctly single-owned by Plan-023 but standalone — no upstream plan deps) and the domain-specific _shell behavior surface_ which depends on Tier 4–7 plans. **Resolution: Plan-023-partial** — the Tier 1 Partial PR Sequence per [Plan-023 §Tier 1 Partial PR Sequence](../plans/023-desktop-shell-and-renderer.md#tier-1-partial-pr-sequence): `apps/desktop/package.json` + `electron.vite.config.ts` + `apps/desktop/src/main/{index,window}.ts` (minimal entrypoint + `webPreferences`-locked window factory) + `apps/desktop/src/preload/index.ts` (single `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld('sidekicks', bridge)` with stub method bodies) + `apps/desktop/src/renderer/src/{main,App}.tsx` (placeholder React mount) + `packages/contracts/src/desktop-bridge.ts` (`SidekicksBridge` interface stub re-exporting Plan-007 daemon types) + `apps/desktop/eslint.config.mjs` (renderer-untrusted import bans) + the `apps/desktop/test/launch.smoke.test.ts` Vitest smoke — lands at Tier 1 alongside Plan-001 to unblock Phase 5 + Plan-024 Phase 3. Resolves [BL-101](../archive/backlog-archive.md#bl-101-c-3--plan-023-tier-1-partial-substrate-carve-out-mirrors-plan-007-partial--plan-008-bootstrap) (option (a); BL-101 closed 2026-04-30 and archived). **Plan-023-remainder** (full main-process behavior — daemon supervisor, keystore, WebAuthn, auto-updater, DAG fallback, deep-link, crash reporter, build pipeline + fuses + signing + notarization + ASAR digest, full Playwright `_electron` E2E suite, CI gate, release runbook) ships at the original Tier 8 slot. Tier 8 placement of Plan-023-remainder is unchanged because the heavy main-process behavior depends on Plan-007 (daemon contract) + Plan-018 (PASETO tokens) + Plan-008 (control-plane tRPC client) + Plan-024 (PtyHost contract for the supervised daemon). The Plan-001 Phase 1 placeholder `apps/desktop/src/index.ts` (shipped 2026-04-26 in PR #6) is repositioned to the substrate split during Plan-023-partial per its forward-declaration comment ("split into `apps/desktop/src/{main,preload,renderer}/` per the electron-vite zero-config convention" — comment amended in this PR for path-convention consistency with the new architecture-canonical authority).

This carve-out follows the same substrate-vs-namespace decomposition rule as Plan-007 + Plan-008 above: the _package skeleton substrate_ (workspace package + toolchain + minimal entrypoints + bridge stub) is what Plan-001 Phase 5 + Plan-024 Phase 3 consume; the _shell behavior surface_ is what Plan-023 owns canonically. Substrate ships first; behavior ships at the owning plan's tier when its upstream dependencies (Plan-007 / Plan-018 / Plan-008 / Plan-024) are in place. **Architecture-canonical paths — orchestrated 5-axis research synthesis.** The carve-out adopts the electron-vite zero-config convention `apps/desktop/src/{main,preload,renderer}/` per [container-architecture.md §Canonical Implementation Topology](./container-architecture.md#canonical-implementation-topology) and [component-architecture-desktop-app.md §Implementation Home](./component-architecture-desktop-app.md#implementation-home). This canonicalization is grounded in an orchestrated 5-axis primary-source research synthesis (60+ sources surveyed by parallel Opus 4.7 subagents, 2026-04-30): **(A)** electron-vite official Development guide ([electron-vite.org/guide/dev](https://electron-vite.org/guide/dev)) explicitly prescribes `src/{main,preload,renderer}/` as the zero-config-detection layout; **(B)** production OSS app survey (17 apps including Cherry Studio, Chatbox, DeepChat, Feishin, PicGo, Deskreen, LobeChat, Marktext-rewrite) finds 9–10 of 14 verified apps using this layout; **(C)** 22 articles + talks 2024–2026 unanimously back this layout from maintainer-grade sources (alex8088, Spatie Ray, daltonmenezes, Tuui, Forge docs); **(D)** none of the seven major monorepo tools (Turborepo, Nx, pnpm, Yarn, Lerna, Bun, Rush) publish official Electron templates that would override this; **(E)** layout is orthogonal to security posture (Electron security docs, Electronegativity SAST, and security-conscious app layouts impose no constraint). The pivot supersedes (a) Plan-023's earlier `apps/desktop/electron/{main,preload}/` naming, (b) the BL-101 description's `apps/desktop/main/src/...` self-reference, and (c) an interim `apps/desktop/{shell,renderer}/` architecture-canonical that this PR amends in lockstep. The full research artifact set lives transiently under `.agents/tmp/research/electron-app-layout/{A-toolchain-docs,B-production-apps,C-articles-talks,D-monorepo-tooling,E-security-hardening}.md` (gitignored, surface-forward to the [BL-101 archive entry](../archive/backlog-archive.md#bl-101-c-3--plan-023-tier-1-partial-substrate-carve-out-mirrors-plan-007-partial--plan-008-bootstrap) per AGENTS.md transient-research convention).

### Plan-025 Substrate-vs-Namespace Carve-Out (Tier 1 / Tier 7)

Plan-025's `Dependencies` header declares Plan-018 as a dependency (PASETO issuer key publication), but Plan-018 cannot compile without `packages/crypto-paseto/` which Plan-025 owns (`Plan-025 §Risks And Blockers`). Plan-002 Phase 2 has the same problem for invite-token issuance per CP-002-4 (BL-119). **Resolution: Plan-025 Tier 1 Partial** — create `packages/crypto-paseto/` exporting the PASETO v4.public `signV4Public`/`verifyV4Public`/`generateV4PublicKeyPair` AND v4.local `encryptV4Local`/`decryptV4Local` primitives (no symmetric-key generator ships — v4.local callers supply 32 random key bytes; built on `@noble/curves` Ed25519 + `@noble/ciphers` XChaCha20 stream — v4.local is encrypt-then-MAC with BLAKE2b-MAC, **not** XChaCha20-Poly1305 AEAD — + `@noble/hashes` BLAKE2b-MAC + BLAKE2b-KDF per [ADR-010 §PASETO v4 Implementation Library](../decisions/010-paseto-webauthn-mls-auth.md#paseto-v4-implementation-library)), `KeyRing` rotation surface, and the PASETO RFC conformance vector suites for both primitives — lands at Tier 1 alongside Plan-007-partial / Plan-008-bootstrap / Plan-023-partial. **Plan-025 remainder** (Fastify relay server, `@fastify/websocket` frame handling, PASETO verification middleware, Plan-021 rate-limiter wiring, operator HTTP surface, graceful shutdown, structured logging, config loader, Dockerfile, `docker-compose.yml`, Caddyfile, CI release workflow, operator runbook, and all eight Spec-027 secure-defaults rows) waits for Tier 7 after Plan-008-remainder + Plan-018 + Plan-021 are ready to instantiate. This carve-out follows the same substrate-vs-namespace decomposition rule as Plan-007 + Plan-008 + Plan-023 above: the _crypto primitive substrate_ is what Plan-002 Phase 2 + Plan-018 consume; the _relay deployment surface_ is what Plan-025 owns canonically and ships at Tier 7. BL-119 (option (a), substrate-vs-namespace) resolved 2026-05-20; Plan-002 + Plan-018 dep headers cite "Plan-025 Tier 1 Partial" — header-alignment propagation completed inline with the BL-119 PR.

### V1.1+ Plans (Out Of V1 Tier Set)

Plans below are not part of V1 per ADR-015 and are **not** placed in the numbered tier sequence above. Their tier placement will be decided at V1.1 plan-authoring time, against the then-current V1 build state.

No plans currently deferred to V1.1+ tier set. (Plan-017 was promoted to V1 per [ADR-015 Amendment 2026-04-22](../decisions/015-v1-feature-scope-definition.md#amendment-history) / BL-097; placed at Tier 8 above.)

### V1.1+ Cross-Plan Extensions

Extensions to an existing V1 surface that are deferred to V1.1, each gated on a primitive a different plan must ship first. Unlike the deferred plans above, these are not whole plans — they are scoped additions recorded here so the gating plan surfaces the waiting consumer at V1.1 planning time, and so the gate is not lost between the deferring decision and the plan that lifts it.

| V1.1 extension | Owned by | Gated on (provider) | Decision record |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Node-scope anchor control-plane witnessing** — upload of sentinel-partitioned (daemon-scope) Merkle anchors to `event_log_anchors`, so a remote / compliance auditor can verify node-scope security-posture chains (in V1 these are witnessed locally only). | Plan-006 (`event_log_anchors`, anchor-upload path) | **Plan-018 / Plan-003** — a non-forgeable node-identity key + control-plane resolution surface (a sessionless chain has no participant roster to resolve the anchor-signing key from; a self-provisioned node key is forgeable by the local operator it would attest against). When that primitive ships, this extension unblocks. | [ADR-017 §Node-Scope Anchor Witnessing](../decisions/017-shared-event-sourcing-scope.md#node-scope-anchor-witnessing-v1-local-only-v11-control-plane-upload); [Spec-006 §Daemon-Scope Event Binding](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#daemon-scope-event-binding-and-node-scope-anchoring) |
| **Independently verifiable trust path for `signing_key_slot_conflict`** — an authentication channel for the T4.10 registrar's conflict alarm that does not rest on the very key the roster refused. In V1 the alarm row is signed with the refused daemon's local key, so a roster-resolving external verifier sees it only as one more `signature_mismatch` — the wholesale mismatch IS the external signal, and the durable row serves local diagnosis plus the anchor-covered post-repair forensic record. | Plan-006 (the T4.10 registrar conflict-append; the `audit_integrity_failed` sixteenth mode) | **Plan-018 / Plan-003** — the same non-forgeable node-identity key + control-plane resolution surface the row above waits on: a node-scoped attestation key independent of the refused session-scoped signing slot is the trust root that authenticates the conflict record without trusting the refused key. | Adjudicated 2026-08-01 by the Plan-006 T4.10 targeted readiness-audit delta, carried from the PR #274 round-6 review; [Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (`audit_integrity`)](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#audit-integrity-audit_integrity) (the sixteenth-mode prose records the V1 scoping + this deferral) |
| **Cross-session `key_reuse_detected` observation beyond the daemon-local horizon** — a control-plane-side detector over the full `daemon_signing_public_keys` roster, closing the structural blindness Plan-006 I-006-4-03 records: a daemon-local observer detects a collision only where at least one colliding identity is locally resolvable, so a collision spanning two sessions with no common member is invisible to every V1 observer. V1 deliberately REJECTS control-plane uniqueness enforcement — under global uniqueness an attacker registering a victim's readable public roster key into an attacker-owned slot would permanently lock the victim out (no V1 rotation ceremony exists), and the probe reintroduces the key-existence oracle the register path's refusal collapse prevents — so the V1.1 shape is attributable observation, never registration-time refusal. | Plan-006 (`key_reuse_detected` taxonomy + the I-006-4-03 observable-scope invariant) | **Plan-018 / Plan-003** — the same node-identity + control-plane resolution primitive: an authenticated node-identity roster is what turns a control-plane-side observation into attributable, non-oracle testimony rather than an anonymous key-presence lookup surface. | Adjudicated 2026-08-01 by the Plan-006 T4.10 targeted readiness-audit delta; [Plan-006 §Invariants](../plans/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#invariants) I-006-4-03 (the observable scope, the rejection rationale, and the fail-closed both-identities halt) |

### Spec-024 Implementation Plan

[Plan-027: Cross-Node Dispatch And Approval](../plans/027-cross-node-dispatch-and-approval.md) owns the V1 implementation of [Spec-024](../specs/024-cross-node-dispatch-and-approval.md). Earlier Session H-interim dependency headers on Plans 002/003/008/012 remain historical context for surfaces governed by Spec-024, but those plans do not absorb cross-node dispatch implementation work.

Plan-027 owns the `cross_node_dispatch_approvals` and `cross_node_pending_dispatch` Local SQLite tables, the `cross_node_dispatch_coordination` Shared Postgres table, the daemon/control-plane dispatch modules, and the typed dispatch contract. Its Tier 9 placement keeps it after the relay, identity, approval, replay, crypto, and desktop approval surfaces it composes.

### Optimization Notes

- Plan-003 (Tier 3) has Plan-001 as its only hard plan-level upstream — Plan-008-bootstrap is already at Tier 1 (already in place at Tier 1) and the Plan-006 taxonomy edge is a Tier 4 additive follow-up (Plan-003 Tier 3 ships event-shape stubs only for the integrity envelope; the per-event payload-shape schemas are Plan-003 Phase 2, and the Tier-4 follow-up is union-registration + `EventEnvelope` + `CapabilityDetails` binding). Plan-003 could move to Tier 2 alongside Plan-002 if parallelism is desired.
- Plan-014 (Tier 7) depends only on earlier-tier substrate — Plan-006 (Tier 4 — `EventLogService.append` + the T1.2 type registry) + Plan-007-partial (Tier 1 — the `artifact.*` IPC `MethodRegistry`), both complete before Tier 7 (see the §3 Plan-014 row) — and has no within-Tier-7 upstream, so it could move as early as Tier 5 (once Plan-006 lands at Tier 4); its Tier-7 placement is conservative. Plan-012 (Tier 6) cannot move earlier: its Phase-2 trust-envelope evaluation reads Plan-009/Plan-010 envelope roots, and its event/wire surfaces ride Plan-006 (Tier 4) + Plan-004 (Tier 5) (Tier-6 audit; see the §3 row).
- Within Tier 6, Plan-010 depends on Plan-009. If Plan-009 finishes before Plan-012, Plan-010 can start immediately without waiting for Plan-012.
- Plan-024 (Tier 1) has no upstream-as-blocker inter-plan deps for Phases 1–3 and could land at any point before Plan-005 begins; placed at Tier 1 to document the standalone status explicitly. Phase 3B (campaign B16) is the scoped exception: consume-only extension seams per CP-024-4 plus the CP-024-5 credential-seam `plan_phase` precondition gating Phase 3B on Plan-006 Phase 3 merged (2026-08-02, PR #279; Phase 3B holds its own Tier-4 row per the same remainder split).

## 6. Active Next Steps DAG

This section tracks the actionable next steps the [plan-readiness-audit Tier 1](../operations/plan-implementation-readiness-audit-runbook.md) PR #15 left for the Tier 1 code track and the Tiers 2-9 audit track. Each `NS-NN` node is one actionable step — code, governance, audit, or cleanup. Edges are hard preconditions; a node moves from `blocked` to `todo` when all incoming edges are satisfied. Existing [`BL-NNN`](../backlog.md) entries are cross-referenced where they gate downstream `NS-NN` nodes. Static plan-level dependencies live in §3; static tier ordering lives in §5; this section is the dynamic "we are here" pointer that complements both — it never overrides them. In particular, an audit node reaching `completed` clears only the **audit gate**: the code lanes it unblocks are not thereby code-ready — they dispatch only once §5 tier order is satisfied (all prior-tier code complete — excepting the procurement-blocked Plan-024 Phase 4-5 tail at NS-09/NS-10, a standalone Tier-1 leaf that per §5's Optimization Notes has no inter-plan deps and so gates no later tier) **and** their own plan §Preconditions are met. Audit clearance is necessary, not sufficient. All of this gates plan-task dispatch; the enhancement and tooling lanes (CONTRIBUTING.md §How Code Lands) sit outside it. Status values follow [`backlog.md`](../backlog.md) conventions (`todo` / `in_progress` / `blocked` / `completed`).

### NS-39: Plan-010 Phase 1 — Worktree Contracts + Persistence

- Status: `completed` (resolved 2026-07-26 via PR #253 — squash commit `f3c9947`: shipped Plan-010 Phase 1 (Contracts + persistence, Tier 6), tasks T1.1–T1.4 — the `worktree.ts` contract domain and the four-table worktree-lifecycle migration, with contract↔DDL lockstep pinned by a conformance tripwire. T1.1 CREATEd `packages/contracts/src/worktree.ts`: the three branded UUID ids `WorktreeId` / `EphemeralCloneId` / `BranchContextId` (minted via the Tier-2 `brandedUuidIdSchema` helper — `BranchContextId` is the CP-010-6 provide-forward to Plan-011), the three Plan-010-owned enum vocabularies (`WorktreeStateSchema` over the six worktree states, `EphemeralCloneStateSchema` over `"creating" | "ready" | "retired" | "failed"`, `CleanupPolicySchema` over `"on_run_complete" | "manual"`), and `WorktreeLifecyclePayloadSchema` as Plan-009 T1.1's state-parameterized `buildRepoWorkspaceLifecyclePayloadSchema` instantiated over `WorktreeStateSchema` — the PR #250 round-4 mechanism, so no third `state` arm ever lands in `repo.ts` — plus the additive registration of the five `worktree.*` lifecycle payload variants into the Plan-006-owned `SessionEventSchema` union per CP-010-5, against the five type names + `session_lifecycle` category rows Plan-006 T1.2's `SESSION_EVENT_CATEGORY_BY_TYPE` census already carries. The event set is closed by construction, not by convention: the `-> failed` transition deliberately emits no worktree event and clone transitions emit none at all (D-010-11 / I-010-13), and a `worktree.failed` literal is pinned REJECTED by the union — the registration-is-not-an-emission-license rule NS-37 states, read in the other direction. T1.2 EXTENDed the same file with the seven wire request/response pairs implementing `api-payload-contracts.md §Plan-010` verbatim (`ExecutionModeSelect`, `ExecutionRootPrepare`, `WorktreeReuseCheck`, `EphemeralClonePrepare`, `EphemeralCloneDispose`, `WorktreeRetire`, `WorktreeStatusRead`), strict-parsed so a TTL-like extra key on clone prepare is rejected rather than silently dropped (the wire carries no TTL — D-010-2), with `RunId` threaded as a top-level `import type` from Plan-005's `provider-driver.ts` for the status read's `createdByRunId` provenance field and its runtime `runIdSchema` re-derived locally from the same brand rather than imported, so `worktree.ts` takes no runtime module edge on `provider-driver.ts`. T1.3 CREATEd `packages/runtime-daemon/src/migrations/0004-worktree-lifecycle.ts` — `worktrees`, `ephemeral_clones`, `branch_contexts`, `run_execution_contexts` plus seven indexes (two partial-unique: the `idx_worktrees_active_branch` at-most-one-live-checkout race arbiter for I-010-4, and the `idx_branch_contexts_worktree_workspace` D-010-15 upsert key) and six `CHECK` constraints — transcribed verbatim from the ratified DDL in [local-sqlite-schema.md §Workspace and Git Tables (Plan-009, Plan-010, Plan-011)](schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md#workspace-and-git-tables-plan-009-plan-010-plan-011) (D-010-5: the audited DDL there IS the migration content), inlined as an exported TypeScript string constant on the `0001-initial.ts` precedent, and registered through Plan-001's migration-runner guarded-block seam as schema version 4. Its `REFERENCES repo_mounts(id)` / `REFERENCES workspaces(id)` clauses forward-reference Plan-009 Phase 2 tables no migration creates yet: SQLite does not check parent-key definitions at `CREATE TABLE` time and defers the error to statement preparation (primary source cited at `Plan-010 §References`), so the migration applies cleanly on a parent-less database and Phase 1 carries no build-order dependency on Plan-009 Phase 2 (the ratified campaign order — the first insert path opens at Plan-010 Phase 2, which machine-gates on that merge; a premature INSERT fails as `SQLITE_ERROR: no such table`, not as a foreign-key violation, because with the parent table absent SQLite never reaches constraint evaluation). T1.4 CREATEd `packages/runtime-daemon/src/git/__tests__/contract-ddl-conformance.test.ts` — the I-010-2 lockstep tripwire, and the **first file in the Plan-010-owned `src/git/` subtree**, which therefore opens a phase earlier than CP-010-7's service list implied. It pins four vocabularies, not three: the migration additionally `CHECK`s the Plan-009-owned `run_execution_contexts.execution_mode`, and the comparison splits by ownership — the three Plan-010-owned vocabularies compare as ordered **sequences** (a reorder there is a Plan-010 authoring change the tripwire must catch), while `execution_mode` compares as an unordered **set**, because a Plan-009 reorder is a wire non-event: RFC 8785 JCS serializes the literal string, and ADR-018 admits only additive MINOR evolution of the vocabulary. NS-39 is a §6 DAG sink at minting — the anchored repo-wide check `grep -rn '^- Upstream:.*NS-39' --include='*.md'` returns zero matches — so born-`completed` it promotes nothing. The anchor is load-bearing and this sentence originally omitted it: an unanchored search for the bare string matches THIS line, so the loose form can never return zero and could not have established the claim it was cited for. Re-derived from post-edit §6 state rather than carried forward from NS-38: the ready set is **{NS-20}** at this node's resolution (the Tier 8 plan-readiness audit, then the sole dispatchable §6-tracked lane — the housekeeping PR that follows mints NS-40 and widens it to **{NS-20, NS-40}**), the blocked set is **{NS-09, NS-10, NS-21}** (unchanged — NS-09/NS-10 procurement-blocked on BL-108, NS-21 behind NS-20 in the serialized audit chain), and the completed set gains NS-39, moving from 34 to 35 of the 39 NS nodes the DAG held at that point — 40 once NS-40 mints (NS-13a and NS-13b count separately; NS-27 has no §6 node; the BL-108 governance node sits outside the NS census). Its merge satisfies the `Plan-010 Phase 1 merged` leg of Plan-010 Phase 2's own precondition, whose other two legs — Plan-009 Phase 2 merged and Plan-006 Phase 3 merged — are both unmet, so Phase 2 is not dispatchable and is not a §6 NS-node yet; it mints at dispatch per the NS-30 auto-create precedent, in the mint-at-own-merge shape NS-34 established. Plan-010's plan-level status stays `approved` — only Phase 1 reaches `completed` here; Phases 2-5 remain and the Tier-6 audit checkbox stands. by the established "tier-K audit → first code phase" edge precedent (`NS-14 → NS-24`, `NS-15 → NS-30`, `NS-16 → NS-35` + `NS-16 → NS-37`, `NS-18 → NS-38`) it gates Plan-010's first code phase) + NS-38 (Plan-009 Phase 1 — repo contracts + root resolution, merged 2026-07-26 via PR #250: the CP-010-1 import origin. `worktree.ts` imports `ExecutionMode`, `WorkspaceState`, the branded `RepoMountId`/`WorkspaceId`, and the `buildRepoWorkspaceLifecyclePayloadSchema` factory from `repo.ts`, and `WorktreeLifecyclePayloadSchema` **is** that factory instantiated over `WorktreeStateSchema`, so the phase cannot compile until `repo.ts` is on `develop` — the hard build-order edge NS-38's own entry names as the `Plan-009 Phase 1 merged` leg of this phase's precondition, machine-enforced as `plan_phase, plan: 009, phase: 1, status: merged`) + NS-37 (Plan-006 Phase 1 — event taxonomy + envelope contracts, merged 2026-07-25 via PR #247: the `SessionEventSchema` union-registration seam T1.1 EXTENDs with the five `worktree.*` payload variants per CP-010-5, against the census rows Plan-006 T1.2 already carries; The NS-37 edge lands **direct, not transitively through NS-38**: the existing `NS-37 → NS-38` hop encodes NS-38's own six `repo.*`/`workspace.*` registrations, and Plan-010's five `worktree.*` variants are a sibling additive EXTEND of the same Plan-006-authored seam that nothing in NS-38's shipment produces — the author-of-the-consumed-symbol test NS-38's Upstream applied when it landed NS-30 direct rather than riding `NS-30 → NS-31 → NS-37`. Recorded because the alternative is defensible on reachability alone: NS-37 is reachable via NS-38, so a reachability-only reading would elide the edge — the tipping constraint is that retiring or re-scoping NS-38 would then silently drop Plan-010's Plan-006 dependency, which the machine-enforced precondition block asserts independently. Plan-010's remaining plan-level deps carry no §6 NS-edge of their own — Plan-001's daemon SQLite substrate (the migration-runner guarded-block seam + `openDatabase`, shipped at Tier 1, which predates the §6 DAG) and its `SessionIdSchema`, the Tier-2 `brandedUuidIdSchema` package-internal helper at `packages/contracts/src/internal/branded.ts` (consumed substrate-style, the treatment NS-38 gave it), and the Plan-007-partial / Plan-004 / Plan-023-partial surfaces whose IPC, run-gate, and renderer legs ride Phases 2-4. The NS-35 edge above was recorded at minting as a deliberate **non**-edge and is retracted by the PR #254 review round — the reasoning conflated two different concerns. `import type` is elided at emit, so `worktree.ts` takes no RUNTIME module edge on `provider-driver.ts` (which is why T1.2 re-derives `runIdSchema` locally instead of importing it), but the §6 code track encodes BUILD order, and a type-only import blocks `tsc` with `TS2307: Cannot find module` exactly as hard as a value import does. Runtime-edge absence is a module-cycle property, not a build-order one; the original rationale's other leg — that the §3 Plan-010 row declares no Plan-005 dependency — described a §3 gap rather than a §6 fact, since Plan-010's own T1.2 **Consumes** has named `RunId ← Plan-005 Phase 1` since the Tier-6 audit (that §3 row now carries the clause too). Note the NS-37 precedent does NOT settle this either way: `event.ts` imports `DRIVER_CAPABILITY_FLAGS` / `IdempotencyClassSchema` as VALUES from the same file, so `NS-35 → NS-37` was always a runtime edge — the distinction drawn here was real, just not load-bearing for build order. One non-edge remains deliberate. **Plan-009 Phase 2 is not an edge** even though T1.3's FK clauses name tables it creates: unchecked parent-key definitions at `CREATE TABLE` time make the reference forward-only, and the real gate sits on Plan-010 Phase 2's precondition block.

(JCS)](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8785) (the other half of that split's rationale: the canonical bytes carry an enum member's literal string, so a Plan-009 reorder of `execution_mode` is invisible on the wire and `execution_mode` compares as an unordered set, while the three Plan-010-owned vocabularies compare as ordered sequences), [SQLite — Foreign Key Support §5 "CREATE, ALTER and DROP TABLE commands"](https://www.sqlite.org/foreignkeys.html#fk_schemacommands) (the primary source for T1.3's forward-FK premise: "The parent key definitions of foreign key constraints are not checked when a table is created. There is nothing stopping the user from creating a foreign key definition that refers to a parent table that does not exist" — such errors "prevent the application from preparing SQL statements that modify the content of the child or parent tables", and the message "can also be 'no such table' if the parent table does not exist"; confirmed 2026-07-26)
- Summary: Ship Plan-010 Phase 1 — the `worktree.ts` contract domain plus the four-table persistence layer every later phase reads and writes. `packages/contracts/src/worktree.ts` (T1.1–T1.2): three branded ids, three Plan-010-owned enum vocabularies, `WorktreeLifecyclePayloadSchema` as Plan-009's lifecycle-payload factory instantiated over the worktree state vocabulary, the CP-010-5 additive registration of the five `worktree.*` variants into `SessionEventSchema`, and the seven strict-parsed wire request/response pairs. `packages/runtime-daemon/src/migrations/0004-worktree-lifecycle.ts` (T1.3): `worktrees` / `ephemeral_clones` / `branch_contexts` / `run_execution_contexts` with their provenance columns, partial-unique race arbiter, polymorphic at-most-one-root CHECK, and mode-conditional identity CHECK, applied as schema version 4 through Plan-001's guarded-block seam. `packages/runtime-daemon/src/git/__tests__/contract-ddl-conformance.test.ts` (T1.4): the I-010-2 tripwire holding contract enums and migration `CHECK` clauses in lockstep, opening the Plan-010-owned `src/git/` subtree. Contracts + schema only — no git services, no execution-root orchestration, no IPC namespace, no renderer; those are Phases 2-4, and the turn-snapshot service is Phase 5.
- Exit Criteria: T1.1–T1.4 merged; the contracts suite green over the worktree enum/brand/wire coverage and the widened `SessionEventSchema` union tests (including the D-010-11 pin that a `worktree.failed` literal is rejected and the exact-per-family pin that a base-vocabulary state is rejected by `WorktreeLifecyclePayloadSchema`); the migration applying on a fresh database and idempotent under the runner's guard, with the partial-unique active-branch index, the `branch_contexts` at-most-one-root CHECK, and the `run_execution_contexts` mode-conditional CHECK each verified accept-and-reject; the I-010-2 conformance tripwire green across all four pinned vocabularies with its mutation canary failing as designed. **Met.** (PR #253.)
