### NS-41: Plan-006 Phase 2 — Crypto Protocol Core

- Status: `completed` (resolved 2026-07-27 via PR #256 — squash commit `faff8cc`: shipped Plan-006 Phase 2 (Crypto protocol core, Tier 4), tasks T2.1–T2.5 + T2.7, opening the Plan-006-owned `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/` subtree; 29 files, +11985/−142. T2.1 CREATEd `canonicalizer.ts` — RFC 8785 JCS canonical bytes behind the phantom-branded `CanonicalBytes` type, over an **exact** `canonicalize@3.0.0` pin rather than a caret range: the canonical bytes are the input to a hash chain, so a patch-level serialization change is a silent chain break across every previously-signed row, not a bug fix — and EXTENDed the Plan-006-owned `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` in place with `EVENT_ENVELOPE_SEQUENCE_MAX`, bound on `EventEnvelopeSchema.sequence` via `.max()`: above `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` distinct sequences collapse onto one IEEE-754 double and the canonical bytes stop being injective, so two different events could canonicalize identically and collide in the `row_hash` chain — a hash-collision guard, not an arbitrary limit, and not raisable. T2.2 CREATEd `signer.ts` — the BLAKE3 hash chain rooted at `GENESIS_PREV_HASH` (32 zero bytes) plus Ed25519 sign/verify pinned to strict RFC 8032 semantics via `{ zip215: false }`, so a signature that ZIP-215's looser cofactored equation would admit is refused. T2.3 shipped the golden-vector suite binding the canonicalizer to **RFC 8785 Appendix B Table 1** (Appendix A is the ECMAScript sample canonicalizer and carries no vectors), alongside the NUL / lone-surrogate / callable-`toJSON` refusal fixtures. T2.4 CREATEd `pii-indirection.ts` — the injected `PiiEncryptor` seam and the `PiiPayloadCiphertext` brand, and the **sole** PII write path: cleartext never reaches `payload`, and both the ciphertext's BLAKE3 digest and the PII owner's participant id are projected into the signed bytes. T2.5 shipped the post-shred signature-verification property suite — a row whose per-participant key has been destroyed still verifies, because the signature commits to the ciphertext and never to the plaintext, which is what makes [Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#shred-fan-out) Path 1 chain-safe. T2.7 CREATEd `signing-key-source.ts` — the `DaemonSigningKeySource` interface plus `OsKeystoreSealedDaemonSigningKeySource`, which generates the per-session Ed25519 keypair and seals the private half through an INJECTED `DaemonSigningKeySealer` boundary rather than sealing it itself — and `migrations/0005-daemon-signing-keys.ts`, registered as schema version 5 through Plan-001's migration-runner guarded-block seam. **T2.6 was DELETED during the review rounds, not shipped**: the genesis-row backfill migration it carried is gone and its task id is retired, never reused (recorded in Plan-006 §Notes, 2026-07-26). Five Codex rounds closed a single recurring class in exactly three instances — a **signed-but-unchecked binding**, where the signature commits to a value nothing ever compares against the column it stands for: `occurred_at_not_canonical` (I-006-2-09 / I-006-2-10 — §Canonical Serialization Rules normalizes `occurredAt` many-to-one, so the signature commits to the instant and never to the stored spelling), `pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound` (I-006-2-11 — the signed digest never recomputed against `pii_payload`), and `pii_owner_stamp_unbound` (I-006-2-12 — the signed owner claim never compared against the durable `session_events.pii_participant_id` stamp). Each landed as a pure, total, never-throwing exported predicate — `isCanonicalOccurredAt` in `canonicalizer.ts`, `isCiphertextDigestBound` and `isPiiOwnerStampBound` in `pii-indirection.ts`, each sited beside the mint so check and mint cannot drift apart — plus one new `failureMode` arm on `audit_integrity_failed`. The enum grows **11 → 15** across this PR's rounds: the twelfth, `signature_placeholder`, came from the separate T2.6-deletion round's fail-closed rule for a row whose `daemon_signature` is still the 64-zero-byte placeholder, and the thirteenth through fifteenth are the recurring class above; all four pair with `failurePath: 'signature'`, which names the verification GUARANTEE that failed rather than the column the defect occupies. **Every predicate is deliberately unwired at this commit.** Phase 2 is the provider and emits no verdict: T4.1 (Phase 4) is the registered consumer that runs the two PII predicates as POSTconditions of a green `verifyRow` — the claims live inside the bytes that check authenticates — and runs `isCanonicalOccurredAt` outside `verifyRow` entirely, over the column bytes AS STORED, because `verifyRow` is handed already-canonicalized bytes and never sees the stored spelling. Governance carried alongside the code: invariants I-006-2-09..I-006-2-12 minted; CP-006-11 registered for the unowned `DaemonSigningKeySealer` seam (Plan-022 ships the sealer and specifies the `daemon_signing_keys.sealed_private_key` byte format); CP-006-12 widened to bind Plan-022 to preserve BOTH `pii_payload` and the owner stamp across a shred; and `session_events.pii_participant_id` registered as a §1 Contested Table row above. NS-41 is a §6 DAG sink at minting — the anchored repo-wide check `grep -rn '^- Upstream:.*NS-41' --include='*.md'` returns zero matches — so born-`completed` it promotes nothing. The anchor is load-bearing, on the reasoning NS-39 records: an unanchored search for the bare string matches THIS line, so the loose form can never return zero and could not establish the claim it is cited for. Re-derived from post-edit §6 state rather than carried forward from NS-40: the ready set is **{NS-20, NS-40}** (unchanged — the Tier 8 plan-readiness audit and the Plan-010 targeted re-audit delta), the blocked set is **{NS-09, NS-10, NS-21}** (unchanged — NS-09 / NS-10 procurement-blocked on BL-108, NS-21 behind NS-20 in the serialized audit chain), and the completed set gains NS-41, moving from 35 to **36** of the DAG's **41** NS nodes (NS-13a and NS-13b count separately; NS-27 has no §6 node; the BL-108 governance node sits outside the NS census). Two downstream surfaces name this merge and neither is promoted by it. Plan-022 Phase 2's precondition is two-legged — Plan-022 Phase 1 merged AND Plan-006 Phase 2 merged (T2.4 ships the `PiiEncryptor` interface and `PiiPayloadCiphertext` brand that phase's `pii-codec.ts` implements, per CP-022-1 / CP-006-1) — and this PR satisfies the second leg only: Plan-022's §Shipment Manifest records `shipped: []`, and Plan-022 Phase 1 is itself gated on Plan-007 Phase R2, so Plan-022 Phase 2 stays blocked on the unmet leg and enters no ready set. Plan-006 Phase 3 (the append path, where `EventLogService.append` becomes the sole write path) is the other consumer. Neither is a §6 NS-node yet — they mint at dispatch per the NS-30 auto-create precedent, in the mint-at-own-merge shape NS-34 established. Plan-006's plan-level status stays `approved` — only Phase 2 reaches `completed` here; Phases 3-4 remain and the Tier-4 audit checkbox stands. (Superseded in part 2026-07-29: the CP-006-7 two-leg amendment's T4.10 new-contract-surface growth flipped Plan-006 `approved → review` and re-opened that checkbox — every lane-1 dispatch, Phase 3 included, waited on the T4.10 targeted readiness-audit delta restoring `approved` — delivered 2026-08-01 via PR #278 (NS-42); the sentence records status at NS-41's minting. See [Plan-006 §Rollout Order](../plans/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#rollout-order).))
- Type: code
- Priority: `P1`
- Upstream: NS-37 (Plan-006 Phase 1 — event taxonomy + envelope contracts, merged 2026-07-25 via PR #247: the hard build-order edge, and the only one this node carries. `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/canonicalizer.ts` imports `EventEnvelope` — T1.3's named `EventEnvelopeSchema` over the canonical eleven-member set — as the very type it canonicalizes, and `pii-indirection.ts` imports `EventCategory` + `EventEnvelope` from the same surface, so no file in the phase typechecks until `event.ts` carries the Phase-1 widening. T2.1 additionally EXTENDs that file in place, binding `EVENT_ENVELOPE_SEQUENCE_MAX` onto `EventEnvelopeSchema.sequence`. Consume-and-extend-in-place of an NS-37-authored symbol is the author-of-the-consumed-symbol test at its cleanest: the edge lands where the symbol was authored.) Three further candidates were considered and are recorded as deliberate **non**-edges. **NS-16 (Tier 4 plan-readiness audit) is not a direct edge** — it rides transitively via `NS-16 → NS-37`, following NS-36, the other Phase-2 code node, whose Upstream lists only its prior phase (NS-35) and not the tier audit; the "tier-K audit → first code phase" precedent (`NS-14 → NS-24`, `NS-15 → NS-30`, `NS-16 → NS-35` + `NS-16 → NS-37`, `NS-18 → NS-38`) is by its own terms about the FIRST phase, and the audit checkbox it represents gates the plan, not each successive phase. **NS-35 (Plan-005 Phase 1) is not an edge** — it likewise rides transitively via `NS-35 → NS-37`; no file under `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/` imports `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts` in any form, value or type, so the build-order-vs-runtime-edge distinction NS-39's Upstream had to draw does not arise here. **NS-39 (Plan-010 Phase 1) is not an edge**, and this one is worth the reasoning because the artifact as merged genuinely does depend on it: `migrations/0005-daemon-signing-keys.ts` took schema version 5 only because Plan-010's `0004-worktree-lifecycle.ts` had already taken 4, and `packages/runtime-daemon/src/session/__tests__/migration-shape.test.ts` as shipped anchors the `schema_version` set at `[1, 2, 3, 4, 5]`, which does not hold without Plan-010's guarded block. The constraint is on the migration's NUMBERING, not on the phase's dispatchability: reverse the merge order and Phase 2 numbers its migration `0004` with nothing else changed. §6 edges are hard preconditions — the §6 preamble defines a node as moving from `blocked` to `todo` when all incoming edges are satisfied — and Plan-006 Phase 2 was dispatchable before Plan-010 Phase 1 merged, so drawing the edge would assert a precondition that never existed. This is a merge-order artifact of a shared numbering sequence, the same shape as NS-39's own deliberate Plan-009-Phase-2 non-edge, where the FK clauses name tables the phase does not wait on. Plan-006's remaining plan-level deps carry no §6 NS-edge of their own — Plan-001's daemon SQLite substrate (the migration-runner guarded-block seam + `openDatabase`, shipped at Tier 1, which predates the §6 DAG) and its `SessionIdSchema`, threaded by T2.7's per-session key store — the same treatment NS-37, NS-38 and NS-39 each gave it. **Plan-002's amendment-via-extension is not a gate on this phase either**: the §1 `daemon_signing_keys` row above and CP-006-7 name a session-create `DaemonSigningKeySource.create(sessionId)` call-site, but [Plan-006 §Rollout Order](../plans/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#rollout-order) anchors that gate at **step 4 (Phase 3), not step 2** — T2.7 ships the source and nothing in Phase 2 signs an appended row, so the phase merged with the amendment still outstanding, as designed. (CP-006-7 was re-vehicled 2026-07-29 — the call-site now binds at the first production composition root, leg A, and the roster write is Plan-006 T4.10's post-attach control-plane registration, leg B; the non-edge conclusion recorded here is unchanged, and the wording above reflects the row as it read at NS-41's minting.)

Appendix B Table 1 supplies T2.3's golden vectors), [RFC 8032 — Edwards-Curve Digital Signature Algorithm (EdDSA)](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8032) (the strict verification semantics `{ zip215: false }` selects over ZIP-215's cofactored equation), [ADR-004](../decisions/004-sqlite-local-state-and-postgres-control-plane.md) (the SQLite-local-state boundary keeping the sealed private key off shared Postgres), [ADR-017](../decisions/017-shared-event-sourcing-scope.md) (the shared event-sourcing scope the hash chain and anchor protocol sit inside)
- Summary: Ship Plan-006 Phase 2 — the crypto protocol core every later Plan-006 phase and Plan-022's shred fan-out build on, as a self-contained provider with no wiring. `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/` (new subtree): `canonicalizer.ts` (RFC 8785 JCS canonical bytes behind the phantom-branded `CanonicalBytes`, over an exact serializer pin), `signer.ts` (BLAKE3 hash chain from `GENESIS_PREV_HASH` plus strict RFC 8032 Ed25519), `pii-indirection.ts` (the injected `PiiEncryptor` seam, the `PiiPayloadCiphertext` brand, and the sole PII write path with ciphertext digest and owner stamp projected into the signed bytes), `signing-key-source.ts` (per-session keypair generation with the private half sealed through an injected `DaemonSigningKeySealer`), and `migrations/0005-daemon-signing-keys.ts` applied as schema version 5 through Plan-001's guarded-block seam. `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` takes one in-place EXTEND: `EVENT_ENVELOPE_SEQUENCE_MAX` bound on `EventEnvelopeSchema.sequence`. Crypto primitives only — no append path, no verifier, no compactor, no replay, no IPC namespace, no renderer; the append path is Phase 3, the verifier T4.1 and the read side are Phase 4. **A predicate is not a verdict:** the three tamper-evidence predicates this phase exports are pure, total and never-throwing, and are deliberately unwired here — Phase 2 supplies the check, Phase 4 runs it and reports the `failureMode`, and the split is what lets the checks ship in the same PR that found the holes they close.

## 7. Maintenance

When adding a new plan:

1. Check the Dependency Graph (Section 3) for any shared tables or package paths with existing plans.
2. Add the plan to the Table Ownership Map (Section 1) if it creates or extends a shared table.
3. Add the plan to the Package Path Ownership Map (Section 2) if it creates or extends a shared directory.
4. Add the plan to the Build Order (Section 5) in the earliest tier where all its dependencies are satisfied.
5. Update the plan's header with a `Dependencies` row listing all inter-plan dependencies.

### Forward-Declared Table Migration Ownership

Schema-first plans (e.g., Plan-022 forward-declaring `participant_keys` and `session_events.pii_payload` onto Plan-001's `0001-initial.ts`) retain authoring ownership of post-V1 migrations against the forward-declared surface. The §1 Contested rows encode this split: Plan-001 appears as "Owning Plan (CREATE)" because it ships the Tier 1 initial DDL, and Plan-022 appears as "Extending Plan(s) (ALTER/USE)" because it originated the schema and therefore owns all subsequent `ALTER TABLE` work. Plan-001 must not author post-V1 migrations against `participant_keys` or `session_events.pii_payload`; that responsibility stays with Plan-022.
